Crucially, a Range Weighter and a Feature Fuser bridge these components, dynamically selecting and fusing relevant ranges into optimal contexts for forecasting. Experiments on six benchmarks confirm OrangeTree achieves the SOTA performance. In MIOL settings, it matches the accuracy of length-fixed models without substantial accuracy degradation or a substantial increase in inference latency typical of heuristic methods. Code is available at this https URL .


### [49] [TQ-HQNet: A Trainability-Aware and Qubit-Efficient Hybrid Quantum Neural Network for Cross-Domain Learning on Heterogeneous Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31908)
*Abdur Rasool, Saba Aslam, Hui Chen, Chayut Bunterngchit*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Hybrid quantum neural networks are a promising direction for noisy intermediate-scale quantum computing, but their practical effectiveness is often limited by poor trainability, rigid circuit design, and weak robustness across heterogeneous real-world tasks. This paper proposes TQ-HQNet, a Trainability-Aware and Qubit-Efficient Hybrid Quantum Neural Network for cross-domain learning on heterogeneous data. The proposed framework combines three mechanisms: 1) adaptive latent compression to map domain-specific features into qubit-compatible representations, 2) trainability-aware search over compact variational circuit candidates, and 3) hybrid fusion of multiple shallow quantum experts to improve robustness without increasing qubit cost excessively. TQ-HQNet is evaluated on four public datasets spanning neurological screening, phishing detection, financial direction prediction, and DNA sequence classification. Across all domains, the proposed model consistently improves over a fixed hybrid quantum baseline, with balanced-accuracy gains of +0.280, +0.170, +0.071, and +0.060, respectively. Ablation and sensitivity studies confirm that gains arise from trainability-aware compact circuit selection and expert fusion, not circuit scaling. Although classical baselines outperform on some tasks, fixed-split results show consistent improvements over the fixed hybrid reference. These analyses support the practical role of compact selection and fusion, with repeated-split and hardware-aware studies left for future work.


### [50] [ReJEPA: Bounded Predictive Regulation for Latent World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31910)
*Fangzhou Lin, Yuping Wang, Shuo Xing, Yang Chen, Yuwen Heng, Xin Li, Wenbin He, Yuhan Yao, Zhengzhong Tu, Liu Ren*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Latent world models learn representations by predicting future states in an abstract embedding space rather than reconstructing raw pixels. When training across multi-frame sequences, however, not all frames and spatial regions are equally informative or predictable. We argue that forcing a world model to minimize every prediction error may be unnecessary and harmful. Under standard $L_2$ loss, unbounded gradients force the encoder and predictor to overfit noisy or transient frames at the expense of core physical structure, while amplifying gradient conflict with representation regularizers. Conversely, $L_1$ loss bounds large gradients, but its constant gradient magnitude near zero disrupts the local smoothness required for planning. We resolve this tension with \textbf{ReJEPA}, a bounded predictive regulation principle that combines bounded gradients for large errors with smooth quadratic behavior near zero, instantiated simply via the element-wise $\log\cosh$ loss. As a drop-in training replacement across Temporal Straightening, LeWorldModel, and V-JEPA~2.1, $\log\cosh$ consistently outperforms $L_2$, a curvature-matched quadratic control, $L_1$, and Smooth-$L_1$ baselines without altering architectures or inference procedures. Notably, the gains grow with environmental complexity, scaling from simple navigation to contact-rich manipulation and real-world autonomous driving on NAVSIM. Probing and geometric analyses confirm that relaxing the penalty on extreme errors yields representations that better capture physical states and align with the true planning cost landscape.


### [51] [Meta-Amortized Cognitive Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31914)
*Jerry M. Huang, Lukas Schumacher, Niek Stevenson, Mischa von Krause, Stefan T. Radev*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Cognitive modeling is highly iterative. Researchers repeatedly revise model structures, parameterizations, covariates, and experimental designs as theories evolve and data accumulates. Amortized Bayesian inference with neural networks provides rapid estimation for individual models, but every revision requires new simulations and training. Here, we introduce CogFormerV2, a meta-amortized framework that enables researchers to specify, revise, and fit cognitive models with a single network, carrying amortization across an entire space of models all at once. Trained once in about a day on a single GPU, it delivers accurate, well-calibrated posteriors across more than 14.6 billion model configurations spanning binary, multi-alternative, and continuous decisions, with varying parameter sets, covariate structures, trial counts, and contamination levels. It can fit empirical data from distinct behavioral tasks and extends to new models through continual learning and representation transfer. Released as an open-source interactive application, CogFormerV2 provides a reusable infrastructure for meta-amortized inference in cognitive modeling, while carrying the potential to accelerate computational modeling workflows at large.


### [52] [Rethinking Universal Time Series Foundation Models from Unified Representations to Shared Dynamics (UniTFU)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31921)
*Shuxin Li, Yingjie Zhou, Le Zhang, Jingyuan Xia, Shengxi Li, Ce Zhu, Anderson Rocha*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) have attracted increasing attention in cross-domain forecasting. Existing works mainly rely on large-scale pre-training and general modeling paradigms. However, the former heavily relies on the diversity and domain coverage of pre-training data. Meanwhile, the latter learns unified representations across heterogeneous domains without explicitly identifying the temporal regularities shared among them, limiting robust cross-domain generalization. This work explores cross-domain generalization by characterizing commonalities in recurring local dynamics across domains, rather than relying solely on implicitly learned temporal representations. To capture such cross-domain commonalities, we propose Time Series Foundation Units (TFUs) as reusable building blocks discovered from multi-scale temporal segments, each characterizing a recurring local dynamic pattern shared across domains. Building on TFUs, we develop a unified time series foundation model (UniTFU) that explicitly reuses and composes shared temporal regularities across domains for forecasting. Experiments on seven public datasets show that UniTFU substantially outperforms existing TSFMs in cross-domain forecasting and can efficiently adapt to target-domain dynamics by updating its reusable components with limited target data. UniTFU also achieves strong forecasting performance compared with state-of-the-art methods.


### [53] [Beyond the Last Loop: Reinforcement Learning with a Teacher Extrapolated from the Model Itself](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31939)
*Linghua Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Looped language models apply one shared transformer stack several times per token, and every loop can be read out through the shared language-model head. Post-training them with verifiable rewards is hard: the outcome reward carries one bit per response, spreading it over loops trains intermediate loops on tokens sampled from the last loop, and distillation needs either a teacher that ignores the reward or an external, larger model. We observe that a looped model already contains a teacher stronger than its last loop: the next-token distribution extrapolated from the first loop through the last. ELGO (Extrapolated-Loop Group-relative Optimization) adds to GRPO a dense per-token distillation term towards this distribution. The teacher is computed from an anchor copy of the model, which stays frozen and is replaced by the current model once the student's KL to the teacher falls below a threshold, so the teacher improves with the student without chasing its own extrapolation. The outcome reward splits probability between correct and incorrect responses, and the teacher decides which tokens are preferred inside a response. Between refreshes the objective is KL-regularised RL anchored at the teacher, whose optimum tilts the teacher by the reward. On Ouro-1.4B-Thinking, ELGO raises the average avg@8 over five math benchmarks from 62.32 with GRPO to 64.20 and is the best compared method on every benchmark, while spreading the reward over loops falls below the base model.


### [54] [Sounding the Black Box: Online Training of Embedded Neural Networks in Forward-Only Physical Simulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31964)
*Ruiqi Shu, Tianrun Gao, Haomin Wen, Xiaomeng Huang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Hybrid physics-neural simulators embed neural networks in partial differential equation (PDE) solvers to model unresolved subgrid dynamics while preserving resolved physics and conservation laws. Because closure outputs feed back across time steps, single-step offline regression does not directly minimize multi-step rollout error, motivating online trajectory optimization. Solver backpropagation through time (BPTT) is the standard online solution when solver derivatives are available, yet many operational Fortran and C/C++ simulators expose only non-differentiable, forward-only interfaces. We introduce FORGE (Forward-Only Response and Gauss-Newton Estimation), an online framework that trains embedded networks from forward runs alone by decoupling exact network derivatives from black-box simulator dynamics. Stage I warms up the rollout via a low-dimensional Gauss-Newton step along output-orthonormal weight directions, canceling multi-layer feature drift via a final-layer correction. Stage II perturbs spatial output fields to fit a gain-bounded causal linear response operator across rollout steps, solves for regularized target outputs, and updates all weights via network backpropagation. Across five 2D and 3D benchmarks spanning phase separation, reaction-diffusion, fluid and MHD turbulence, and ocean circulation, FORGE outperforms gradient-free, reinforcement-learning, and emulator-based baselines, cutting coarse-solver test mean squared error (MSE) by 50%-96% in 2D and 70% in 3D, outperforming the strongest forward-only baseline by 16%-69%, and recovering 88%-98% of Solver BPTT's gains. Code is available at this https URL .


### [55] [On the Information Loss of Multi-Token Prediction: Origin and Solution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31966)
*Tangyu Jiang, Zhanke Zhou, Haodi Wang, Yiu-ming Cheung, Xiaohua Jia, Bo Han*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Multi-token prediction (MTP) accelerates autoregressive decoding through block-wise prediction, but remains vulnerable to error accumulation across sequentially generated blocks. To investigate this limitation, we analyze the distinction between block-wise and sequence-level cumulative information loss (CIL). Specifically, when the model generates using its own previous outputs without access to reference answers, the input-conditional gap equals the cross-block dependence of the reference targets. To improve prediction when these generated prefixes are imperfect, we propose Dual-MTP, which supervises shared prediction heads under full and randomly masked contexts. Together, complete-prefix supervision and context perturbations provide complementary training signals while retaining the MTP architecture. To characterize this training objective, we use the normalized dual loss to bound the divergence between the model's predictive distributions under full and masked contexts. Experiments across five backbones and six tasks spanning mathematical reasoning and GLUE understanding show that Dual-MTP achieves the highest average task score on four of five backbones, with gains of up to 2.46 points over MTP. On mathematical reasoning tasks, Dual-MTP also achieves 1.4 to 1.9 times the decoding throughput of the NTP reference. Together, these results show that dual-context supervision can improve prediction accuracy while retaining efficient multi-token decoding.


### [56] [Predict Backbone, Graft Dynamics: A Decoupled Paradigm for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31970)
*Lin Zhang, Zilin Li, Jingmei Zhao, Jiayang Wu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Modern long-term forecasters are typically optimized for point-wise accuracy, which rewards dominant low-frequency backbones but often suppresses high-utility local dynamics such as abrupt variations, transient surges, and turning points. We propose Predict Backbone, Graft Dynamics, a decoupled forecasting paradigm that separates stable structural prediction from grounded dynamic restoration. We instantiate it as GRAFT, where a base forecaster predicts the reliable trajectory backbone, and a dedicated grafting path restores historically recurrent dynamics through retrieval-based evidence, phase-aware release, channel-wise control, and restoration-cooperative optimization. Unlike generic post-hoc revision methods that correct residual errors, GRAFT formulates the second stage as grounded dynamic grafting, where historical response evidence is calibrated into transferable dynamics and selectively grafted onto the backbone under phase-, channel-, and confidence-aware constraints. Across the full evaluation grid of seven benchmarks, two metrics, four horizons, and six backbones, GRAFT improves over the corresponding backbone in 87% of all comparisons, with average MSE/MAE reductions of 4.6%/3.9%. Dynamic-window analyses show that GRAFT restores local dynamics while preserving the stable backbone structure. These results suggest a new principle for high-utility forecasting: predict the backbone, then graft the dynamics.


### [57] [Metric-Dependent Scaling Laws for Sketched Multiclass Softmax Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31995)
*Ziyan Chen, Linhe Zheng, Peilin Liu, Zhongzhu Zhou, Ding-Xuan Zhou*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We study scaling laws for full-batch gradient descent with general stable deterministic learning rates in sketched multiclass softmax regression. In a Gaussian teacher student model with power law feature and teacher spectra, the student observes a random feature sketch and reuses a finite labeled sample throughout optimization. Sketching generally makes the student misspecified, so the analysis must jointly account for approximation, nonlinear finite time learning, and the fixed empirical perturbation induced by data reuse. On an exponentially likely sketch event, we show that cross entropy excess and excess zero one risk share a common spectral scale, consisting of a resolution term and an effective learned dimension divided by sample size; teacher student decision disagreement follows the square root of that scale. The resulting balance gives rate optimal sketch and cumulative horizon choices, and synthetic experiments support the predicted regimes and metric dependent exponents.


### [58] [ADE: A Zero-Training Unlearning Method for Latent World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31998)
*Sirui He, Chi Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Latent world models learn compact recurrent dynamics from offline trajectories, yet deployed simulators can retain backdoor triggers or stale transitions from outdated physical regimes. Existing unlearning methods fail in autoregressive physics: gradient updates distort latent manifolds and compound rollout drift, naive output zeroing suppresses valid control responses on trigger onset, and single-step deletion leaves corruption inside recurrent hidden states. We propose Analytical Dynamics Excision (ADE), a deterministic, zero-training closed-form unlearning algorithm for latent world models. Using only cached pretraining moments, ADE recovers retain covariances via single-pass matrix downdating without raw retain data and cascades closed-form edits across the input bottleneck, hidden states, and downstream heads. Pairing orthogonal excision with whitened recurrent damping and mean-preserving affine calibration neutralizes out-of-manifold backdoors and realigns in-manifold shifts without variance collapse. Across four regimes on three MuJoCo benchmarks, ADE outperforms seven tuned baselines on DreamerV1, eliminating HalfCheetah and Hopper velocity-flip triggers $4.4\times$ faster than fine-tuning, and transfers zero-shot to DreamerV3.


### [59] [To Critic Or Not To Critic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32013)
*Jason Wei, George Pu, Jingxuan Fan, Yuan Emily Xue, Yuan Christy Li*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: As large language models tackle longer and more complex tasks, understanding when a critic improves the efficiency of reinforcement learning (RL) becomes increasingly important. Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) trains a critic to reduce variance and provide fine-grained credit assignment, while Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) drops the critic and avoids the bias of bootstrapping from the critic's value estimates. Although recent critic-based methods have reported strong performance relative to GRPO, it remains unclear when each approach is preferable. We argue that there is no universal answer; instead, the choice depends on several interacting factors, including batch size, critic reliance, and task complexity. We study the bias-variance tradeoff in terms of gradient direction, measuring how closely each method's gradient estimates align with the true policy gradient as batch size grows. Under a shared actor objective, we compare GRPO with PPO in control tasks, short-horizon math, and long-horizon terminal agents. Across more than 1,200 training runs, including 96 with LLMs, we find that PPO generally leads at small batch sizes, while GRPO often overtakes heavily bootstrapped PPO and reaches parity with lightly bootstrapped PPO at larger batches. At longer horizons and in more complex domains, GRPO needs larger batches to catch up to PPO's performance. Accounting for training compute, critics pay off at small budgets, but their returns diminish at larger budgets.


### [60] [Structure-Aware Transformers for Hierarchical Authorization in Personal AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32018)
*Dongyu Cui, Jie Ding, Xuan Bi*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Personal AI agents can carry out complex, multi-step tasks on users' behalf, but doing so often involves numerous permission requests. Manually reviewing each request is time-consuming, while indiscriminate approval poses privacy and security risks. Predicting users' authorization preferences offers a way to reduce this burden while preserving user control. However, authorization prediction is challenging because permission requests are often hierarchical and observed user decisions are sparse and potentially correlated. To address these challenges, We propose the Parent-gated Authorization Transformer for Hierarchies (PATH) to predict users' authorization preferences for personal AI agents. PATH is a novel neural network architecture that combines hierarchy-aware attention masks with a parent-gated probability formulation to ensure hierarchy-consistent predictions. Hierarchy-level embeddings further support learning from sparse observations and patterns shared within each authorization level. We establish finite-sample risk bounds and provide sufficient conditions for consistent authorization probability estimation and algorithmic convergence. Experiments on simulated data, AI-generated user profiles, and real user authorization data demonstrate strong predictive performance with zero hierarchy violations.


### [61] [PFedHASNet: A Personalized Federated Hybrid Analog-Spike Network for Multi-Subject Motor Imagery EEG Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32019)
*Peijia Li, Ruidong Ma, Xianghong Lin, Xiangwen Wang, Guoyong Shi, Shuai Huang, Hongqi Li*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Motor imagery electroencephalography (MI-EEG) data are often collected and stored separately across subjects or institutions, and EEG acquisition and annotation remain costly. Training models solely on individual subject data limits the use of information from other subjects, whereas centralizing raw EEG recordings raises privacy and data governance concerns. Federated learning (FL) enables collaborative model training across distributed clients without sharing raw EEG data, providing a practical approach to leveraging information from multiple subjects. Accordingly, we propose PFedHASNet, a personalized federated hybrid analog-spike network for MI-EEG decoding. The shared encoder integrates continuous-valued temporal features, leaky integrate-and-fire (LIF) spike responses, and channel-wise log-variance statistics through learnable fusion weights. Client-private residual adapters and classifiers perform subject-specific feature refinement and personalized prediction, respectively. Experiments on held-out trials from participating subjects across four public MI-EEG datasets show that PFedHASNet achieves the highest mean client macro-F1 among 18 model configurations in cross-model comparisons, while maintaining competitive decoding performance with a compact client-side model in comparisons using the same backbone. Ablation studies further examine the contributions of hybrid representations and personalization components to decoding performance.


### [62] [Scale-Robust and Global-Aware Representation Learning for Multivariate Time Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32033)
*Gyuwon Lee, Saejoon Park, Won-Seok Hwang, Bogoan Kim, Yunyong Ko*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Multivariate time series anomaly detection (MTSAD) is an essential task for identifying abnormal system behaviors and supporting reliable operations across diverse real-world applications. Despite substantial advances in MTSAD, two key challenges in representation learning remain underexplored: (C1) how to learn scale-robust temporal representations that capture anomalies across different temporal scales and (C2) how to learn global-aware relational representations that capture inter-series dependencies by incorporating series-level global characteristics beyond local windows. To address these challenges, we propose STAGER, a novel method that jointly learns two complementary representations: scale-robust temporal and global-aware relational representations. Specifically, STAGER employs (1) a scale-robust temporal encoder that constructs a multi-scale temporal directed acyclic graph (DAG) to learn scale-robust temporal representations and (2) a global-aware relational encoder that incorporates learnable series-level global embeddings into the temporal representations and models inter-series relationships through graph neural networks. Two complementary auxiliary objectives further enhance the distinctiveness of the global embeddings. Extensive experiments on three real-world benchmark datasets demonstrate that STAGER achieves the best average rank among ten evaluated MTSAD methods across three evaluation metrics. Ablation and sensitivity analyses validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and auxiliary objectives, while efficiency evaluations demonstrate that STAGER achieves a favorable trade-off between detection performance and inference time compared with existing methods.


### [63] [Online Environment Repair for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32037)
*Sheng Lu, Rui Mao, Zewei Chen, Chihua Ying, Beiduo Chen, Dingxin Hu, Tinghao Yu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Programmatic verifiers provide scalable reward signals for training coding agents with reinforcement learning, yet subtle harness defects and incomplete unit tests allow policies to earn full credit without solving the intended task. Existing training-time defenses intervene either by penalizing suspicious outputs or by filtering flagged tasks and rollouts. However, output penalties leave the underlying verifier exploitable, driving policies to disguise shortcuts under reasoning monitors or zeroing out valid solutions under detector false positives, whereas task and rollout filtering discards or mutes the hardest problems where shortcuts emerge earliest. Pre-training verifier hardening repairs the environment directly, but misses vulnerability classes that surface only as the policy improves during reinforcement learning. In this paper, we propose online environment repair, which leaves triggering rollout rewards unchanged to remove hiding incentives and asynchronously patches exploited environments during training. Candidate repairs triggered by execution invariants or differential fuzzing are deployed only after passing three automated validation gates that block the exploit while preserving valid solutions, canceling detector false positives and enabling structural propagation across repositories without scaffold labels or reference solutions. Across 20 controlled GRPO experiments on heterogeneous software-engineering repositories, online environment repair reduces cumulative reward hacking from 36.9% to 0.06% and improves held-out Pass@8 from 21.7% to 46.7%, consistently outperforming reward-penalty, rollout-filtering, and static-hardening baselines while eliminating zero-shot exploitation on unseen loophole classes.


### [64] [Spec-CoFrGeNet: Reparametrizing Continued Fraction Networks for Stable and Accurate Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32041)
*Soumyadeep Pal, Amit Dhurandhar, Vijil Chenthamarakshan, Dennis Wei, Karthikeyan Natesan Ramamurthy*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Continued fractions have been shown to be a function class capable of constructing expressive yet parameter-efficient neural networks. These neural networks are not only effective for supervised learning, but also for training LLMs. However, the rational form of this function class introduces poles when the denominator approaches zero, which prior work has avoided by enforcing a small positive (non-smooth) lower bound. We show that performance and training stability can be highly sensitive to the choice of this lower bound. In this work, we introduce a novel transformation of the partial denominators, inspired by its connection to the Green's function in physics, that mitigates the above issue elegantly by constraining the spectra of the constructed tridiagonal Hamiltonian. Our approach further eliminates the need for incremental training used by previous works for stability, thus avoiding involved modifications to standard pre-training procedures. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our reparameterization by replacing FFNs with a previously proposed continued fraction architecture for both language and image generative modeling. Across diverse models, such as GPT-2 XL, (nano) Llama-MoE, and Granite, we show that our proposed method achieves performance comparable to the original (FFN-based) architectures while maintaining ~30%-50% parameter savings as before, and in most cases even outperforms the more complicated incremental training baseline. We further demonstrate, for the first time for continued fraction networks, that their effectiveness also carries over to image generative modeling, that utilize diffusion objectives. On a state-of-the-art Diffusion Transformer, we show that our approach again achieves performance comparable to the original FFN-based models with ~30% parameter savings and significantly outperforms the previous incremental training approach.


### [65] [Integral DeltaNet: Improved Linear Attention with Adaptive Memory Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32063)
*Junsoo Oh, Jihun Yun, Junhyuck Kim, Hanbaek Lyu*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Linear attention replaces softmax attention with a linear formulation, enabling linear-time computation with a fixed-size recurrent state instead of a growing key-value cache. These computational and memory advantages have attracted growing interest in linear attention for long-context language modeling. However, compressing past information into a fixed-size state causes memory collisions that hinder long-context understanding. Although DeltaNet mitigates memory collisions through delta-rule updates and Gated DeltaNet extends this approach with gating, these mechanisms may also discard information relevant to future predictions. To address this limitation, we introduce Integral DeltaNet, which maintains two complementary recurrent states: a fast-updating delta state that captures current key-value associations and an integral state that preserves a weighted history of the delta state. At readout, the model adaptively combines these states to access information across different timescales. We further derive a chunkwise parallel formulation that enables hardware-efficient training. We evaluate Integral DeltaNet using 340M and 1.3B parameter models trained from scratch on 15B and 100B FineWeb-Edu tokens, respectively. Comparisons with strong recurrent baselines show improved overall performance in language modeling, commonsense reasoning, real-world and synthetic retrieval, long-context understanding, and length extrapolation. We also compare training throughput and activation memory, as well as decoding latency and memory.


### [66] [Fix How It Acts, Not How It Thinks: Surgical Fine-Tuning of Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32064)
*Sandeep Singh Sandha, Prann Bansal, Arvind Sundararajan*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Open-weight LLMs are competent software engineers, but they sometimes fail at tasks they are otherwise able to do. Often it is not a failure of reasoning. It is a few small missteps over a long trajectory (repeating a step that has already failed, or declaring the task done while a requirement is still unmet) that make the difference. Correcting this calls for a surgical weight update that steers the model toward the right actions without interfering with its reasoning. Such updates, typically learned with LoRA adapters, are often too small to survive merging into a released checkpoint. In our BF16 release, the median weight change is only 1.5% of a single rounding step, and merging loses roughly two thirds of the update's norm. When the same adapter is merged into the mixed FP8/NVFP4 release of the same model, 93% of the update disappears. Instead of increasing the size of the update, we directly train on the surviving part. We introduce merge-aware fine-tuning. Standard LoRA rounds the merged weights to the storage format only at export, so the loss never sees the rounded weights. We instead round the sum of base weights and adapter update to the release format at every training step, keeping the vendor's quantization scales, and compute the loss on these weights. The forward pass matches the serving engine numerics, and the gradient passes straight through the rounding, so the model we train is the model we ship. The loss optimizes only the model's actions and keeps its reasoning close to the base model. Applied to Nemotron-3.5, the recipe improves agentic coding in both the BF16 and the NVFP4 release, by up to 8.4 points on Terminal-Bench 2.1, and we publicly release both models.


### [67] [A Fused Gaussian Process Bandit Framework for Multi-Fidelity Bayesian Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32092)
*Muyun Lu, Jose E Aguilar Escamilla, Huazheng Wang, Ying Lin*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Bayesian optimization (BO) is widely used to maximize expensive black-box functions when direct evaluation is costly. In many real-world applications, the high-fidelity objective can be approximated by a series of lower-fidelity models that provide partial but informative signals at substantially reduced cost. Multi-fidelity BO (MFBO) algorithms aim to strategically combine these information sources to achieve an optimal trade-off between accuracy and evaluation cost. However, existing MFBO methods often oversimplify the complex dependencies between fidelity levels or rely on heuristic acquisition functions that lack theoretical performance guarantees. To address these limitations, we introduce a new formulation of the MFBO problem as a fused Gaussian Process bandit and develop a corresponding Fused Gaussian Process Upper Confidence Bound (FGP-UCB) algorithm. Our approach accurately predicts the high-fidelity function by fusing multi-fidelity observations through an autoregressive Gaussian Process model and explicitly quantifies the uncertainty arising from parameter estimation across fidelity levels. The proposed method also provides theoretical simple-regret guarantees, overcoming a key limitation of existing MFBO algorithms that lack provable performance bounds. Effectiveness of the proposed method is demonstrated via both theoretical analysis and empirical studies.


### [68] [PhysRF-WorldJEPA: Transforming Heterogeneous Large Language Models into Manifold-Decoupled Millimeter-Wave Radar World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32113)
*Yicheng Huang, Feng Ke, Linhan Chen, Ziyi Jiang, Xiu Yin Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Adapting Large Language Models (LLMs) to millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar enables privacy-preserving, all-weather physical scene understanding across darkness, smoke, and walls. However, standard instruction tuning treats continuous radar streams as static text-like tokens, causing near-random global radar-to-text retrieval and severe physical drift under sensor blackouts. Conversely, coupling pairwise contrastive losses (e.g., SigLIP) directly into the shared backbone improves retrieval but injects $O(B^2)$ repulsive gradients that fracture continuous coordinate manifolds ($R^2 < 0$). We present PhysRF-WorldJEPA, a manifold-decoupled Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) that separates continuous electromagnetic state prediction from cross-modal language alignment. An unattenuated 1.0x clock anchor and parallel dual-scale RF encoder feed a lean ~3% LoRA LLM trained with two-step state-space EMA-JEPA (Denoise -> Rollout) and sample-axis Batch-VICReg to predict +3.0 s physical states; a stop-gradient alignment head then maps frozen prompt states onto the text hypersphere with false-negative masking. On a 4,541-window tri-domain suite (77/60.25/60 GHz) against 10 baselines, freezing the physical backbone and training only the decoupled head lifts R->T R@10 from 5.95% to 15.01% and improves MedRank from 107.0 to 60.0 while preserving identical physical readout: 3.37 deg ($R^2=+0.961$) blackout +3.0 s torso tilt, 1.89 m ($R^2=+0.679$; 1.19 m/+0.863 at the RF front-end) cluttered UAV altitude, 2.04 m ($R^2=+0.461$) behind-wall NLOS range, 0.9976 virtual-RIS invariance, and 100% fall recall. Across five 0.5B-1.8B LLM families (15 zero-overhead models), World-JEPA improves retrieval and 75%-drop completion in 10/10 runs and blackout tilt in 4/5 (Span-Euc), 2/5 (Scatter), and 5/5 (per-family best) over SFT.


### [69] [Monte Carlo Tree Search for Classifier Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32118)
*Romain Poggi, Jesse Read*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Multi-label classification continues to be relevant to a variety of modern tasks. Among other challenges, modern data sets are increasingly large with respect to the number of labels, and these labels may be noisy or missing in the training sets. Label-wise scores and ranking-based metrics have been popular, but optimising exact multi-label evaluation (where a predicted set of labels should match the true set exactly) is particularly relevant for many domains, e.g., in medicine; alongside some measure of confidence. This requires an estimation of joint probability across labels (a model of label dependence; conditioned on each input instance). A family of methods known as probabilistic classifier chains provides such inference. However, these existing varieties either do not scale to a large number of labels or do so by approximating joint via marginal distributions in a way that compromises the estimation of uncertainty for exact match accuracy. We overcome this issue by proposing TSCC; leveraging a Monte Carlo Tree Search methodology to enhance chain inference via an efficient balance between the exploration of label space and the exploration of promising label-set predictions. Results are promising both in terms of predictive and computational performance.


### [70] [Rethinking Reasoning Effort Training in Large Language Models: On-Policy Distillation or Reinforcement Learning?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32131)
*Tianliang Lv, Hao Li, Xinchao Xu, Zhengkun Zhang, Ju Huo*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Large language models have shown strong capabilities on complex tasks, yet how to control their reasoning effort remains a significant challenge for practical deployment. This paper provides a systematic investigation of reasoning-effort training, contrasting reinforcement learning (RL) with on-policy distillation (OPD). We find that compressing reasoning and separating effort tiers are governed by different signals. Length-penalized RL reliably compresses a single policy, reducing thinking on AIME25 by 70\% without accuracy loss, but collapses the tiers when they are trained jointly. Multi-teacher OPD separates them: across 33 benchmarks, low and medium use 58\% and 43\% fewer thinking tokens than high, while high improves on the reference policy by 3.1 points. We trace this difference to the training signal. A sequence-level reward cannot tell tier-specific behavior from shared behavior, whereas OPD supplies a tier-specific target at every visited state, including the decision to stop. Building on this analysis, we show that a single token-level KL anchor on the high tier is sufficient to learn separated tiers within one RL stage, and that natural-language effort instructions make this separation emerge from the start of training, yielding a policy competitive with multi-teacher OPD at matched thinking length. Finally, the separation built by dense targets holds only on the states seen in training: it does not carry over to unseen agentic tasks, where effort acts mainly through the number of interaction turns, whereas the compression induced by sequence-level rewards does.


### [71] [Calibrated Reading of Latent Reasoning and Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32143)
*Zixi Wang, Gaohao Zhou, Yubo Huang, Xin Lai, Yushe Cao, Jingzehua Xu, Shuai Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Latent reasoning and key-value communication store intermediate information in continuous representations, limiting direct inspection of model behavior. Existing activation readers trade the low cost of task-specific probes against the flexibility of language-model decoders. We propose the Calibrated Latent Reader, CLR, a compact transformer that predicts reasoning outcomes and message content through task-conditioned interactions between states and candidate answers. Source adapters support transfer across models, while conformal calibration provides coverage and false-alarm guarantees under exchangeability. On two CODI models, CLR improves accuracy over MLP and attention probes by 4-18 percentage points for final-answer prediction and reference-value membership. On CODI-GPT-2, it achieves accuracy comparable to a 1.7B LatentQA-based verbalizer using 437-648 times fewer inference FLOPs. On synthetic Qwen3-4B communications, comparing decoded facts with public commitments detects 93.1% of message substitutions at a nominal 5% false-positive rate.


### [72] [RetraQ: Manifold-Constrained Critic Guidance for Flow Policies in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32148)
*Xuanzhuo Liu, Wu Chen, Ruixiang Wu, Shuai Yuan, Chuankang Li, Tongxin Li, Maoqing Yao, Guanghui Ren*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: A learned critic can improve a pretrained flow-matching policy by steering sampled actions toward higher predicted value. However, even a critic that fits the behavior data well can provide misleading action gradients. Gradient components pointing away from the data-supported region are weakly constrained by behavior data and can lead guidance to exploit critic extrapolation errors. We introduce RetraQ (Retraction-based Q-guidance), which uses geometry encoded by the pretrained flow to constrain both the direction and the destination of critic guidance. A single velocity evaluation of the frozen flow defines a denoising map. RetraQ filters the critic gradient with the map's Jacobian and applies the map itself to correct the updated action. Our analysis characterizes when these operations approximate tangent filtering and retraction onto the behavior manifold. The same update guides actions at test time and provides targets for policy distillation and online reinforcement learning. In controlled studies, RetraQ improves true return while keeping refined actions close to the data-supported region, and its distillation targets remain more stable than those of raw critic ascent. In offline-to-online training on LIBERO and OGBench, RetraQ achieves higher mean final success than the evaluated critic-guidance baselines. On a real-world plug-insertion task, test-time RetraQ improves the success rate of a behavior-cloned $\pi_{0.5}$ policy from 55.0% to 76.7%. Online training further increases success to 98.3% in only 30 minutes and 100% within one hour. Project page: this https URL


### [73] [Multi-view Random-set Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32170)
*Mengchi Zuo, Tianyang Wang, Cai Xu, Ziyu Guan, Wei Zhao*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Trusted multi-view learning aims to integrate heterogeneous views for accurate and reliable predictions. However, multi-view data often exhibit the view degradation phenomenon: individual views contain only partial discriminative information and cannot independently determine the instance-level label. Requiring such views to produce unambiguous class evidence can distort evidence learning and mislead subsequent fusion. Existing methods addressing this problem have at least one of the following drawbacks: (1) assigning evidence to individual classes without explicitly representing unresolved class groups; (2) projecting composite beliefs onto individual classes before fusion, preventing direct refinement of candidate groups through cross-view set intersections. To address these limitations, we propose a new Multi-view Random-Set Learning (MRSL) paradigm, which preserves view-specific support for candidate class sets and refines candidate classes through cross-view fusion. Specifically, we quantify class overlap to select a compact collection of sets for each view, avoiding the exponential cost of modeling all class subsets. We then perform set-level fusion by assigning joint support to set intersections, allowing complementary views to narrow the candidate classes before probability conversion. Experiments on synthetic and eight real-world datasets validate the proposed overlap measure and demonstrate that MRSL achieves the highest accuracy on all eight datasets. Compared with the strongest baselines, MRSL improves average accuracy by 4.71% and reduces average expected calibration error by 6.33%. All datasets and code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.


### [74] [Steerable by Design: Conditional Pretraining for Robust Control of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32199)
*Henry Castillo, Yanchen Liu, Claudio Mayrink Verdun, Dylan Hadfield-Menell*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Our goal is to build language models that are steerable by design. Today's language models are pretrained with next-token prediction, which relegates alignment and control to the final 1% of training tokens or inference-time interventions. These models repurpose a single input channel for both instructions and data, leading to common failure modes where the model mistakenly treats data as instructions such as intentional jailbreaks and unintentional in-context alignment drift. To correct this, we propose a pretraining method and architecture that separates the two from the very start, producing Mancelle-3B. An encoder processes high-level semantic features of upcoming text with cross-attention that prevents the token stream from writing back into this control channel. During pretraining, the model learns to rely on these features to predict the next token, turning the channel into an effective handle for steering the model. Mancelle-3B steers more strongly than prompt- and activation-based methods on comparable open models while maintaining fluency, and it withstands adversarial black-box and soft-prompt counter-steering that defeats those baselines. We reproduce this against a more controlled baseline with Mancelle-400M. Our results show that robust control can be built into language models if we do so early and intentionally.


### [75] [Who gets the information? An $α$-fair label-coverage framework for multi-label feature selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32200)
*Zhenzhi Tang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Recent information-theoretic filters for multi-label feature selection disagree on how to distribute relevance over labels: some amplify features that inform a few labels, others balance what every label receives. We show these choices are implicit social welfare functions. Treating labels as agents whose utility is the information they receive, we propose FLIC, which greedily maximises the $\alpha$-fair welfare of per-label information coverage. A single parameter $\alpha$ moves from utilitarian ($\alpha=0$) through proportionally fair ($\alpha=1$) to max-min selection ($\alpha\to\infty$), and a dozen published relevance terms lie on this axis, most as special cases, first-order approximations or limits. The objective is monotone submodular for every $\alpha\ge0$ whenever each label's information is, so greedy selection is a $(1-1/e)$-approximation; this fails for $\alpha<0$, and the max-min limit is inapproximable. Fano's and the Hellman-Raviv inequalities tie raw coverage to the Bayes Hamming loss and normalised coverage to per-label relative error. FLIC uses bias-corrected, label-wise conditional information and has no relevance-redundancy trade-off parameter. On 22 data sets, FLIC is the best of 14 information-theoretic filters on seven of nine metrics, winning 86 of 117 pairwise comparisons significantly and losing none significantly. Among 17 methods it ranks first on ranking loss, coverage and macro-AUC with two classifiers and has the best mean rank (narrowly with ML-kNN), while three embedded methods lead on Hamming loss, one-error and micro-F1.


### [76] [Joint Classification and Generation on Tabular Data: an Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32225)
*Tim d'Hondt, Oleksii Furman, Damian Kwolek, Jan Poręba, Maciej Zieba, Mykola Pechenizkiy*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Many applications of machine learning on tabular data need both a classifier and a generator of realistic records. The default is to train two separate models, whereas a single joint model promises both from one set of weights. Several families of tabular deep learning models can be turned into such a joint model, which raises the question of which joint model is preferable at which computational budget. In this empirical study, we introduce and compare joint models based on four state-of-the-art tabular data modeling families (energy-based models, probabilistic circuits, tabular foundation models and tabular diffusion models) against measured FLOPs and wall-clock time, on 29 classification datasets. Methods are evaluated based on two defined aggregate scores that take into account different aspects of prediction and generation. We then also compare these joint models with every pair of a separate classifier and generator at different computational budgets, to see when a joint model may be a defendible choice against the regular practice of keeping a separate classifier and generator. We find that the best joint model is a probabilistic circuit at the smallest budgets and, beyond that, a tabular foundation model that generates column by column using a regressor (TabICL). Compared with pairs of separate models, this study finds a joint model is rarely preferable but often very close to the best pair, without the hassle of training and maintaining two separate models.


### [77] [Seldon TFM family: Seldon-Mini, Seldon and Seldon+](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32257)
*Stanislas Angebault, Nicolas Atienza, Nicola Cancedda, Aziz Fouché, Salomé Gobbi, Ekaterina Iakovleva, Nicolas Makaroff, Arthur Nahmias, Paul Novello, Alexandre Pasquiou*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We introduce Seldon, a family of tabular foundation models. Our classifier Seldon+ ranks first among 15 models on TabBench, our benchmark of 124 classification datasets, and on its variant with missing values. In our own runs of public benchmarks, it also ranks first on BeyondArena IID classification and TabBench-Bio, and second on TabArena classification. The open-weight models Seldon-Mini (27M parameters) and Seldon (213M parameters) both lie on the TabArena Pareto front of Elo against inference time: no entry is both more accurate and faster. On TabArena, Seldon ranks fourth and Seldon-Mini seventh among 109 leaderboard entries, and on the IID track of BeyondArena, Seldon ranks second among 42 entries. Seldon-Mini and Seldon handle both classification and regression, use an architecture similar to those of Kumo-Tabular, TabFM and TabPFN-3.5, and are trained on synthetic tables only.


### [78] [Pando: Noise-Coupled Parallel Draft Trees for Efficient Edge Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32269)
*Joren Dumoulin, Hyoukjun Kwon, Subhankar Pal, Steven Li, Vignesh Vivekraja, Marian Verhelst*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Autoregressive (AR) decoding emits one token per forward pass, reloading the full model each time, so LLM inference on edge devices is memory-bound while the compute units sit idle. Speculative decoding puts that idle compute to work by verifying several drafted tokens in a single pass. State-of-the-art parallel drafters such as DFlash and DSpark propose an entire block of draft tokens in one forward pass, which makes drafting compute-efficient. However, they are deployed with draft chains whose length is capped by the block size they are trained for, leaving most of the computational budget for verification unused. To tackle this problem, we propose Pando, which builds dynamic draft trees on top of a parallel drafter and thereby removes this cap. Pando employs noise-coupled pruning, which draws the target model's sampling noise before drafting and ranks candidate branches under that noise, so the tree retains the branches the target will actually sample. Noise coupling is lossless and requires no retraining or additional model pass, yet increases acceptance length by up to $8.4\%$ on Qwen3-8B. To optimize and evaluate Pando configurations across hardware, we normalize an operator-level roofline to AR decoding, reducing every platform to its ridge point. On a representative edge device, a 16-node Pando tree attains $5.3\times$ speedup and $4.2\times$ energy reduction over AR decoding: $25\%$ faster than DSpark's released chain, and $27\%$ faster and $72\%$ more energy-efficient than an EAGLE-3 tree. The best configurations remain memory-bound on every edge device we model, so added compute capacity does not reduce their latency.


### [79] [CoMNO: Commutator Memory Neural Operators for Long-Horizon PDE Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32274)
*Han Xiao, Xin Zhang, Yuhan Zou, Zhenzhong Wang, Donglin Cao, Dazhen Lin, Min Jiang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Solving nonlinear partial differential equations (PDEs) over long time horizons is computationally expensive using traditional numerical methods. While neural operators provide an efficient alternative, they suffer from the accumulation of approximation errors along the predicted trajectory, typically caused by the failure to capture fine-scale details. In this work, we design a Commutator Memory Neural Operator that identifies fine-scale dynamical details by commuting two operations and leverages these details to enhance the neural operator's capability in capturing fine-scale features over long time horizons. Specifically, we identify a performance gap between two operations: downsampling the high-resolution field before rollout prediction versus performing rollout prediction before downsampling. We observe that fine-scale dynamical details are often encoded in this gap. By commuting these two operations, the fine-scale details removed during downsampling can be captured. Furthermore, we employ a memory module to store these fine-scale features along the trajectory and retrieve them during rollout, generating spatially adaptive corrections that enhance long-horizon fidelity. Empirical results across five systems demonstrate that CoMNO improves prediction accuracy and long-horizon rollout stability.


### [80] [DAiSy: Online Data Selection by Adaptive Utility Estimation for LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32294)
*Simin Fan, Martin Jaggi*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Model-aware online data selection prioritizes the training documents that contribute the most given the model's current state. Existing online selectors rank candidates upon gradient-based data utility scores, which requires the full forward-backward pass of the main model on every candidate document. Scoring each document therefore pays as much as training on it, yet only a small fraction is used for the real model update. We introduce DAiSy, a lightweight linear auxiliary model that learns to predict data utility scores from frozen document embeddings, which eliminates the computation overhead and infrastructure burdens. The linear estimator is updated alongside the main language model, fitting to the exact gradient-based utility scores obtained from the backward pass of regular training steps. Consequently, scoring a candidate requires only a linear transformation, bypassing the costly gradient-alignment computation. We demonstrate its effectiveness and efficiency for LLM pretraining in English and multilingual scenarios, with various steering target designs. The improvements from DAiSy can consistently survive the instruction-tuning phase.


### [81] [SPRUNN: Structural Proximity for Pruning in Deep Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32317)
*Babak Rokha, Ali Azarpeyvanda, Alireza Khanteymoori, Tara Ghasempouri*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) have demonstrated significant accuracy across various machine learning tasks. However, these networks suffer from a huge number of parameters and computations, which leads to time-consuming processes. Consequently, parameter pruning approaches have been proposed to compress DNNs and efficiently implement them. The main challenge in pruning is selecting the appropriate parameters for pruning without significantly damaging the accuracy. In this paper, we propose SPRUNN, an algorithmic pruning approach based on the structural proximity criterion for filter pruning in convolutional layers of deep neural networks. Structural Proximity (SP) calculates filter similarity by considering all filters and comparing each one with the others, effectively identifying less important filters for pruning in the convolutional layers. Furthermore, we integrate this approach with fine-grained pruning in Fully Connected(FC)layerstocompresstheDNNsthatinclude both convolutional and FC this http URL ,weproposealearning rate refinement strategy during the retraining phase. Experimental results demonstrate that SPRUNN achieves significant compression with high accuracy across diverse architectures and datasets.


### [82] [When Exact Token Importance Is Not Enough: Limits of Top-K Selection in LLM Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32324)
*Irmaan Mirzanejad*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Long-context language models often retain only a limited set of past token positions for an attention query. A common strategy scores positions individually and keeps the highest-ranked K. Yet attention weights are renormalized over the chosen set: two individually important positions need not form the best pair. We ask whether independent ranking suffices even when each importance score measures the exact downstream change in next-token predictions caused by deleting that position alone. It does not. A four-position construction makes the ranked pair incur 133.2 times the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence of the optimal pair, and a parametric family proves that this multiplicative gap is unbounded. An exact exchange identity and preference reversals for every K >= 2 explain the interaction. In exhaustive frozen-model audits of SmolLM2, Gemma, and Qwen, exact-singleton Top-4 leaves at least 10% relative improvement within a fully enumerated 24-position candidate pool in 55/60, 49/60, and 60/60 cases, respectively. SwapCert, a same-budget one-swap audit, detects 157/164 such cases and returns directly checkable improving supports. A KL-margin certificate relates support fidelity to next-token stability; larger-budget, multi-layer cached-decoding experiments measure predictive fidelity at K = 128 and 256. These results establish joint support quality as an essential object of analysis for sparse attention and motivate downstream-aware support selection under inference constraints.


### [83] [Beyond Hard Tokens: Predictive Memory for Multimodal Protein Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32347)
*Xiaoliang Shi, Zichen Wang, Zhijian Wei, Zhanpeng Shi, Zhenghong Zhou, Yunpeng Wang, Shuangjia Zheng*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Multimodal protein language models unify sequence and discrete structural tokens via iterative masked decoding, offering a promising paradigm for joint protein design. However, standard decoders rely on a memoryless mask reset at each iteration: only a sparse subset of newly committed hard tokens is carried forward, while all remaining unresolved sites are reset to an identical [MASK] embedding. In open-ended co-generation where neither modality provides a fixed external anchor, this stateless transition limits single-step refinement depth and deprives both tracks of stable cross-step soft anchors, leading to slow convergence or degenerative repetitive collapse. Observing that uncommitted predictions evolve with pronounced temporal coherence rather than erratic noise, we introduce Predictive Memory (PredMem), a training-free self-conditioning recurrence that carries these soft beliefs across steps. At each step, PredMem summarizes raw logits into a probability-weighted Top-$k$ expectation embedding at every residue and affinely interpolates it into eligible masked inputs on the next step at zero extra denoiser cost. Across DPLM-2, DPLM-2.1, and ESM3, PredMem raises unconditional sequence-structure co-generation success by 3.6, 17.7, and 48.7 percentage points, respectively, while improving the quality-diversity trade-off and suppressing repetitive mode collapse. Controlled diagnostics show that these gains stem primarily from early trajectory stabilization in unanchored cross-modal co-evolution rather than extra compute or simple entropy reduction, establishing PredMem as an efficient inference mechanism for multimodal discrete generation.


### [84] [CriticJEPA: World Models Are Better Critics Than Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32357)
*Xvyuan Liu, Jianjie Fang, Honglin Zhang, Zhaolu Wang, Xin Zhang, Wei Wu, Fengli Xu, Chen Gao, Yong Li*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Visual world models enable goal-directed planning by predicting action outcomes. Gradient-based planners optimize actions to lower predicted goal cost, but continued optimization can exploit prediction errors after execution stops improving. Comparing plans requires informative relative scores; improving them also requires reliable update directions. Exact-state execution reveals that a frozen hierarchical world model ranks goal-conditioned proposals even when its rankings among outputs of prolonged gradient search are uninformative. Effective planning pairs goal-directed candidates with predictions that distinguish their outcomes. We introduce CriticJEPA, which learns goal-conditioned flow proposals from reward-free trajectories and uses frozen rollouts to select among them at every hierarchy level. Forward selection forms a complete planner; short anchored updates refine candidates before selection. Across visual navigation and manipulation, CriticJEPA increases success at a fraction of the planning compute, transferring shared settings across independently trained models, hierarchy depths, and unseen tasks while keeping every world-model weight fixed.


### [85] [From the ELBO to Maximum Likelihood: Convergence of Variational Autoencoders for Nonlinear Mixed-Effects Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32372)
*Jan Rohleff, Johannes Schropp*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Nonlinear mixed-effects (NLME) models are the central statistical models of pharmacometrics, where they support dose selection, individualized dosing and regulatory decisions in drug development. Statistically, NLME modeling is a marginalized likelihood problem. Variational autoencoders (VAEs) solve such problems with a neural network encoder that amortizes the approximation of the intractable posterior distribution, and allow the integration of AI components into NLME modeling. This paper provides the convergence analysis of the VAE-NLME algorithm. First, its version for the evidence lower bound (ELBO) is proven to converge almost surely to the set of stationary points of the ELBO, without assuming that the encoder is trained exactly. Second, the final VAE-NLME algorithm trains the encoder on the ELBO by gradient-based optimization and uses it as proposal for importance-weighted sufficient statistics. It converges almost surely to the set of stationary points of the marginal log-likelihood, without any assumption on the training of the encoder. Thus, it combines the fast gradient-based training of VAEs with the limit of maximum likelihood estimation.


### [86] [Metamorphic Testing of Learned Spatial Surrogates for Transit Accessibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32373)
*Till Zacher, Markus Lienkamp*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Transit-network design requires evaluating many candidates, but detailed accessibility calculations are too slow to repeat inside an optimization loop. We therefore train a neural surrogate to approximate them. Given a candidate bus network and fixed information about rail service and nearby opportunities, it predicts the calculated spatial change in accessibility. We compare its predictions with routing-derived targets in geographically held-out regions and test specified qualitative relations under controlled input changes. Reference calculations on constructed networks assess the applicability of relations that have a routing-level counterpart. Models with positive predictive skill can still fail basic connectivity tests. One targeted intervention substantially increases pass rates for two connectivity relations while predictive skill also improves. Average prediction error is therefore insufficient when a learned model becomes an optimization objective. Predictive agreement and controlled behavioral tests should be reported separately.


### [87] [The Activity Geometry of Discrete Generative Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32380)
*Xinlong Guo, Jie Zhang, Lingyou Zhou*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Iterative refinement is a key capability of diffusion-based generation and has motivated growing efforts to bring similar behavior to discrete diffusion and flow models. A growing line of work enables such refinement by allowing previously generated tokens to be revised during sampling, thereby shaping the coupling between successive sampling states. Despite its growing role in sampler design, this coupling freedom remains insufficiently characterized. We introduce Activity Geometry to study these finite-step couplings through expected transition cost, which we call activity. We show that activity decomposes into the minimum required by the induced marginal transport and an excess term arising from the coupling. Using this minimum as a reference, we propose Minimal-Activity Finite-Time Coupling (MAFTC) to realize prescribed marginals with minimum activity without retraining or additional model evaluations. In the continuous-time limit, the minimum activity per unit time converges to the minimum expected jump rate compatible with the prescribed path. For metric-induced Gibbs paths, we further show that the activity of the kinetic-optimal flux admits a geometric decomposition. We find that total activity alone can obscure coupling excess: a sampler may approach the minimum activity required by the prescribed marginal transport while still retaining substantial coupling excess. In language generation, as the number of model evaluations increases, refinement-based samplers accumulate additional trajectory activity with limited diversity gains, whereas MAFTC reduces trajectory activity while maintaining comparable generation quality and higher lexical diversity.


### [88] [Evolve to Learn: Gradient-aligned experience evolution for model self-improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32390)
*Haoyuan Li, Gangyi Zhang, Dong Wang, Yang Liu, Daoxuan Liu, Jiancan Wu, Shiu-hong Kao, Wu Liu, Yicong Li, Yongdong Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Self-evolving language models can continuously acquire useful experience through test-time interaction, reflection, and prompt optimization, yet turning such experience into model itself to ensure self improvement remains challenging. On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) provides a natural mechanism for internalizing test-time information into model parameters, yet it largely assumes that the gener- ated experience is worth learning. We show that this assumption does not always hold: experiences that improve test-time performance may provide limited or even harmful training signals. To better characterize which experience should be inter- nalized, we systematically analyze diverse mutated experiences across multiple tasks and reflection models, and find that gradient-based alignment is a more reli- able indicator of downstream distillation gains than immediate test-time improve- ment. Based on this finding, we propose E2L, a self-evolution framework that iteratively generates, evaluates, and distills experiences according to their esti- mated training utility. E2L closes the loop between experience generation and parameter learning, enabling agents to autonomously produce experience suitable for self-improvement.


### [89] [Fractal Neural Synthesis: Constant-Storage Procedural Weight Derivation via Complex Quadratic Boundary Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32393)
*Volkan Dagli, Zerrin Dagli, Daghan Dagli*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Conventional neural networks store synaptic parameters as explicit tensors whose persistent Flash footprint scales linearly (O(W)) with parameter count, constraining resource-limited edge microcontrollers. We formulate and systematically evaluate Fractal Neural Synthesis (FNS), a procedural weight derivation architecture that replaces hidden-layer matrices with a constant 24-byte coordinate seed Theta = (c_x, c_y, ell) in F_64^3 anchored near the complex quadratic bifurcation boundary dM of f_c(z) = z^2 + c. We introduce a large-bailout (R = 256) smooth escape kernel, four zero-overhead geometric sampling apertures--Cartesian Grid, coprime 3x3 Rule-of-Thirds (Z/9Z), phi-Vogel spiral, and multi-scale Harmonic Tripod--and prove conditional Kolmogorov (K_Kolm <= 192 + c_0 bits) and Shannon entropy bounds. Across magnifications zeta in [10^0, 10^8], C^1 Fourier and lattice-noise bases collapse to post-standardization rank ~2.25 and SHA-256 PRNG exhibits avalanche discontinuity (rho ~ 0.019, 50.6% micro-step retention), whereas dM uniquely sustains both rank (~10.0) and local continuity (rho in [0.81, 0.97], 89.2% retention). Under leak-free 10-fold CV across four zero-scaler-byte non-linear benchmarks, FNS with an INT8 readout surpasses 0-hidden Linear-INT8 by +3.6 to +10.8 pp while delivering 10.7x-129x total Flash reduction (725x-2,773x hidden reduction) relative to converged Float32 MLPs (90.30% Ionosphere in 285 B; 97.22% Optical Digits in 2,598 B; 97.90% PenDigits in 2,598 B; 87.00% Satimage in 1,570 B).


### [90] [Not All Pairs Align: Caption-Guided Audio-Visual Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32399)
*Omar Emara, Nan Lu, Dima Damen, Hazel Doughty*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: We address the problem of learning audio-visual representations from co-occurring modalities. Multimodal contrastive learning typically assumes that paired observations provide shared views of the same underlying information. While this may be true for datasets collected in constrained environments, it does not always hold. For instance, co-occurring audio and visual signals can differ substantially in their content due to off-screen sounds. Nevertheless, standard contrastive objectives treat all paired samples as equally aligned. In this work, we propose Caption Guided Alignment Aware Loss (CGAAL), a model-agnostic objective that leverages modality-specific captions to estimate semantic agreement between audio and video. CGAAL uses these agreement scores to weight the contribution of each positive pair. A warm-up period and gradual thresholding strategy progressively introduce this alignment-aware training signal. We demonstrate the generality of CGAAL by applying it to three contrastive baselines, consistently improving their performance on audio-visual retrieval.


### [91] [Optimal Transport Meets t-SNE: Integrated Visualization without Batch Effects in OT-tSNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32402)
*Azhar Akhmetova, Edward Eichhorn, Boris Landa, Dmitry Kobak, Sebastian Damrich*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Visual exploration is an integral step in understanding the structure of datasets. However, systematic variation between datasets collected under different conditions, known as batch effects, can obscure shared structure and complicate their joint visualization. Existing methods often rely on cross-batch nearest-neighbor relations to integrate the batches. Yet, these local correspondences can be unreliable for challenging cross-batch geometries. We propose optimal-transport t-SNE (OT-tSNE), which replaces nearest-neighbor-based matching with globally coupled affinities derived from entropic optimal transport. Controlled synthetic experiments demonstrated that nearest-neighbor-based methods can deteriorate, while optimal-transport affinities and OT-tSNE embeddings remain robust. Across seven real single-cell datasets, we benchmarked OT-tSNE against a broad range of integration and visualization methods and found that it achieves a Pareto-efficient trade-off between batch mixing and biological conservation, and integrates batches particularly well.


### [92] [A Framework for Graph Node Symmetric Quantification under Dataset Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32403)
*Alessio Micheli, Alejandro Moreo, Marco Podda, Fabrizio Sebastiani, Domenico Tortorella*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Graph node quantification is the supervised learning task of estimating the prevalence of different classes within an arbitrary sample of unlabelled nodes from a graph, subject to some form of dataset shift. Existing approaches to graph node quantification typically follow the standard aggregative paradigm, i.e., they use labelled nodes from the graph to train an intermediate node classifier, and obtain an estimated class distribution for the sample by aggregating the classifier's predictions for the individual nodes and then correcting the result via some adjustment strategy that accounts for dataset shift. In this work we propose a framework for graph node quantification that does away with both node-level supervision, since its training phase only requires node samples labelled by their class distribution, and node-level predictions, since it estimates class prevalence values directly, without the need of any intermediate node classifier. The framework thus allows symmetric quantification, since (unlike in standard ``asymmetric'' quantification methods) the labelled items used for training and the unlabelled items on which predictions must be issued are of the same type (i.e., node samples). This framework (that we dub GSQ, for Graph Node Symmetric Quantification) allows building pipelines consisting of a node encoder, an embedding pooler, a prevalence predictor, and an optional post-hoc scaling step. These components can be instantiated by choosing from different trainable and reservoir-based encoders, different pooling operators, and different prediction objectives. We evaluate the GSQ framework on binary and multi-class datasets with different levels of homophily, under prior probability shift and under structural covariate shift.


### [93] [See the Answer, Learn to Embed: Learning Universal Multimodal Embeddings from Hindsight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32439)
*Yoonki Cho, Youngju Na, Taegeun Yang, Jinhwan Seo, Sung-Eui Yoon*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Universal Multimodal Embedding (UME) models map heterogeneous multimodal inputs into a shared embedding space, where pairwise similarity supports diverse downstream tasks. Recent reasoning-driven UME approaches use intermediate reasoning to generate additional context for embedding, but often incur extra computation at inference. In this paper, we explore the matched candidate (i.e., the paired target) as an alternative source of context, asking whether a model can "see the answer" during training and "learn to embed" without it at inference. However, direct conditioning on this privileged context can introduce candidate-specific information unavailable from the query alone or cause the candidate-conditioned query representation to collapse toward the candidate embedding. To address this, we introduce Hindsight-Enhanced Multimodal Embeddings (HEME), which learns query-grounded hindsight representations from such candidate context. HEME first trains a hindsight teacher with a hindsight attention mask, mismatched-candidate conditioning, and query-token alignment to limit excessive reliance on the candidate. It then freezes the teacher and distills its hindsight representation and similarity distribution into a standalone student embedder. At inference, the student independently encodes queries and candidates without candidate conditioning or the hindsight teacher. Experiments on MMEB-V2 and the agent and text tasks of MMEB-V3 demonstrate the effectiveness of HEME and show that it enables more efficient inference than explicit reasoning-driven methods.


### [94] [The Teacher Is Already Inside: Self-Distilling Looped Language Models Beyond Their Last Loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32452)
*Linghua Zhang*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Looped language models apply one shared transformer stack several times per token, and every loop is readable through the shared language-model head. Post-training them is hard. Reward-only reinforcement learning needs a verifier and learns from one sparse signal per response; spreading the reward over loops collapses the entropy of every loop; and cross-loop self-distillation, as in LoopOPD, can only teach intermediate loops, because the last loop cannot learn from its own frozen copy: the loss is zero at initialisation. We observe that a looped model already contains a policy that decodes better than its last loop: the next-token distribution extrapolated from the first loop through the last. ELPSD (Extrapolated-Loop Policy Self-Distillation) turns this distribution into a self-distillation target: a frozen copy of the model computes it, and the model's own last loop is distilled towards it on its own rollouts, using the exact reverse KL at every visited prefix. Teacher and student are the same model, so ELPSD needs no reward, no external teacher and no privileged context, and it leaves rollouts and decoding unchanged. We show that the objective pulls the last loop towards the base model's last loop with weight 1+w and away from the base model's first loop with weight w, and that the teacher must stay frozen, since an idealised teacher recomputed from the current model extrapolates without bound. We evaluate ELPSD on Ouro-1.4B-Thinking across five math benchmarks against the base model, GRPO, RLTT and OPSD. Without any reward, ELPSD raises the average avg@8 from 61.15 to 63.69 and is the best method on all five benchmarks, ahead of GRPO (62.32), which uses a verifier, and the self-distillation method OPSD (61.77), which needs reference solutions.


### [95] [When Knowledge-Tracing Rankings Depend on Available History: A Source-Correction and Matched-Information Audit on ASSISTments 2009 and 2015](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32489)
*Devis Wawan Saputra*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: Knowledge-tracing benchmarks often compare models that have different access to a learner's previous responses. We examine how data provenance, history length, and representation affect those comparisons. First, we compare a pinned ASSISTments 2009 sequence mirror against the corrected official release. Of 4,148 learners represented in both, 817 have different ordered skill-response sequences; the mirror also contains fewer labeled events. Second, we retain earlier learner-disjoint model evaluations on the 2009 and 2015 datasets, distinguishing exploratory results from a corrective 2015 replication. Finally, we evaluate a new, nonoverlapping sample of 4,000 ASSISTments 2015 learners using three models constrained to the same one-event or 20-event prior-response window. On 20,592 test responses from 582 learners, the 20-event Brier scores were 0.17667 for count-based logistic regression, 0.17389 for an order-aware logistic model, and 0.17514 for a GRU. The GRU-minus-order-aware difference was 0.00125 (95% learner-cluster bootstrap interval [0.00008, 0.00243]); at one prior event, the corresponding difference was 0.00212 [0.00120, 0.00301]. Earlier comparisons had favored the GRU against simpler history summaries. The newer analysis instead favors a logistic comparator that represents order using the same permitted event stream. These results concern predictive performance on particular historical cohorts and model settings. They neither isolate an architectural effect nor establish generalization to contemporary classrooms.


### [96] [Assessing Robustness to Catastrophic Forgetting in Continual Deep Reinforcement Learning for Cyber-Physical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32496)
*Kim N. Nolle, Ivana Dusparic, Rhodri Cusack, Vinny Cahill*

Main category: cs.LG

Abstract: As the use of reinforcement learning (RL) becomes more widespread in cyber-physical systems, it becomes increasingly important to design RL agents that are able to adapt to changes in their environments in order to maintain safety and reliability. This paper addresses the question of whether different classes of RL algorithm are more robust against forgetting previously learned behaviours, a critical issue in continual learning, in non-stationary environments. We evaluate the robustness of representative examples of three important classes of RL algorithm in a non-stationary autonomous parking environment.
