# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [473] [Machine learning enables full Bayesian inference for spinning extreme-mass-ratio inspirals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31916)
*Bo Liang, Minghui Du, Zhenwei Lyu, Hanlin Song, Jiakai Zhang, Weichen Qin, Chia-Jui Chou, Yi Yang, Jingyi Yu, Ziren Luo, Mingming Sun, Peng Xu, Yue-Liang Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

Abstract: Extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs), stellar-mass compact objects spiralling into massive black holes, promise to measure the masses and spins of the central black holes, reveal the strong-field environment around them and gauge cosmic distances via millihertz gravitational-wave observations. Yet most forecasts for the parameter inference of these systems assume known source parameters, and broad-prior analyses have addressed only simplified versions of the problem. The joint inversion of all 14 parameters of a spinning source from the complete prior has remained unsolved. Here we solve it for the augmented analytic kludge model: generative networks narrow the complete prior and reveal a ring of degenerate spin directions, and the exact detector likelihood determines the posterior. For a simulated one-year LISA-Taiji observation, within one ring sector we obtain, for the first time, a 14-parameter posterior of a spinning EMRI from the complete prior, the most precise yet obtained for such a source from broad priors. The redshifted mass and spin of the massive black hole are determined to two parts in $10^5$, the mass two orders of magnitude beyond any electromagnetic measurement. The compact object's redshifted mass is measured to one part in $10^4$, and its eccentric, retrograde orbit discriminates between capture channels. The 0.8% distance and sub-square-degree localization are sufficient for dark-siren cosmology. Year-long coherent analysis resolves the inspiral driven by radiation reaction itself.


<div id='stat.ML'></div>

# stat.ML [[Back]](#toc)

### [474] [Instance-Dependent Bandit Convex Optimization in One Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31608)
*Felix Breuer, Alireza Bakhtiari, Kevin Jamieson*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study the instance-dependent sample complexity of one-dimensional stochastic bandit convex optimization in the fixed-confidence setting. First, we consider minimizer localization, where the learner seeks to identify a point within distance $\varepsilon$ of the unique minimizer. We introduce a new instance-dependent quantity $\Delta_\varepsilon(f)$, corresponding to the threshold for which the sublevel set of $f-\min f$ has length $2\varepsilon$. We prove a lower bound of order $\Delta_\varepsilon(f)^{-2}$ and give a bisection-style algorithm that matches this rate up to logarithmic factors. We then revisit the simple-regret setting, where the goal is to identify an $\varepsilon$-optimal point. Surprisingly, no comparable instance-dependent improvement is possible: every correct algorithm has sample complexity of order $\varepsilon^{-2}$ on every instance, matching the minimax rate up to constants. Together, these results separate the statistical complexity of minimizer localization from that of simple-regret optimization in one-dimensional convex bandits.


### [475] [Online Statistical Inference for Derivative-Free Constrained Stochastic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31687)
*Jianliang Ye, Sen Na*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study statistical inference for nonlinear equality-constrained stochastic optimization problems in settings where derivative information is unavailable or expensive to obtain. While recent advances in stochastic sequential quadratic programming (SSQP) have enabled efficient online optimization, existing methods either rely on gradient information or suffer from high computational costs. To address these limitations, we develop a sketched derivative-free SSQP (SDF-SSQP) method that simultaneously achieves computational efficiency and valid statistical inference. By leveraging randomized sketching, the proposed method reduces the per-iteration computational cost to $O((d+m)^2)$, where $d$ is the dimension of the decision variable and $m$ is the number of constraints, making it well suited for large-scale problems. To overcome the challenges that both sketching and zeroth-order approximations introduce for statistical inference, we adopt a random-scaling approach that eliminates the need for explicit estimation of the asymptotic covariance matrix. We establish the asymptotic normality of the averaged primal--dual iterates and construct a self-normalized statistic that is asymptotically pivotal. The resulting inference procedure is fully online, requires neither covariance estimation nor matrix inversion, and incurs an additional per-iteration cost of the same order as that of the optimization update. Numerical experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method in terms of both optimization performance and statistical inference accuracy.


### [476] [A General Framework for E-process Stopping Rules in Best-Arm Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31898)
*Donghyun Park, Seohwa Hwang, Junyong Park*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We propose an e-process stopping rule in the best-arm identification problem. The e-process stopping rule is different from the Chernoff stopping rule by its hypothesis structure on the stopping decision. The major benefit of an e-process stopping rule is the flexibility in stopping rule design and in fact, it recovers previously suggested Chernoff stopping rule on several bandit problems. Moreover, we analyze the asymptotic optimality of the $\delta$-PAC algorithm with the decomposition of e-processes. The decomposition separates optimality conditions into three parts; the sampling policy allocation convergence, the efficiency of e-process design, and the bandit problem's inherent stochastic behavior. Subsequently, we prove a sufficient condition for asymptotic optimality in bandit algorithms. For the application of an e-process stopping rule, we derived an asymptotically optimal algorithm for the generalized linear bandit problem.


### [477] [DUST: Directional Uncertainty-aware and Scale-invariant Transfer Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31948)
*Abhisek Chakraborty*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Transfer learning seeks to improve efficiency in a target regression task by borrowing information from related external source dataset(s). Existing approaches achieve this by enforcing Euclidean proximity between the target and source regression parameters or, more recently, by encouraging their directional alignment through angle-based penalties. While such angle-based penalties mitigate the sensitivity of the transfer learning methods to the scale differences between source and target regression coefficients, they still rely on a point estimate of the source regression parameter and thus provide no direct mechanism to incorporate uncertainty in the source information. To that end, we propose a probabilistic framework for transfer learning that operates at the level of a \emph{scale-invariant directional distribution} of the source regression parameter estimate, rather than its point estimate, to enable robust, scale-invariant and uncertainty aware transfer under heterogeneous source information. Specifically, we project the sampling distribution of the source regression estimate onto the unit sphere, thereby extracting its scale-free directional distribution. Transfer from source to target is then induced through a novel \emph{angular cone prior} which shrinks the target regression parameter towards directions in which the source distribution assigns high probability, while allowing its magnitude to be learned exclusively from the target dataset. The proposed construction admits exact finite-sample characterizations of posterior angular concentration and posterior computation remains tractable via careful low-dimensional augmentation in the parameter space.


### [478] [A Minimax Optimal Leave One Out Bound for Bounded Losses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31972)
*Peigan Gao, Jian Qian*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: We study proper leave-one-out prediction with $M$ real-valued experts on $n$ fixed inputs. Each label is hidden in turn, and the learner uses the remaining labels to choose one of the experts' predictions at the held-out input. We determine how closely such a rule can match the average loss of the best single expert on the full sample. For any bounded loss that is a continuous nondecreasing function of absolute error and vanishes at zero, let $B$ denote its maximum value. Over classes of size $M$ and samples on which the best expert has average loss at most $r$, the minimax excess loss is of order $\min\{B, \sqrt{\frac{Br\log M}{n}}+\frac{B\log M}{n}\}$. For each class and loss, one deterministic rule attains this rate for every $r\in[0,B]$. The matching lower bound holds even for randomized proper rules given $r$.


### [479] [EnSurv: Deep Generative Survival Modeling with Application to Kidney Graft Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32127)
*Rajdeep Pathak, Tamalika Koley, Shreyashi Basak, Dipankar Bandyopadhyay, Tanujit Chakraborty*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Kidney transplantation is the optimal treatment for life-threatening end-stage renal disease, providing significant survival advantages over dialysis. Given the severe shortage of donor organs, accurately predicting the time to premature graft failure is an essential clinical task for optimizing organ allocation and tailoring individualized immunosuppressive therapies. This study is motivated by a comprehensive United Network of Organ Sharing (UNOS) dataset containing 243,443 kidney transplant records with complex multi-region structures and high right-censoring rates. We propose EnSurv (survival engression), a deep generative survival framework designed to capture complex, non-linear clinical covariate interactions for modeling time to graft failure. Within a pre-additive noise model (pre-ANM) family, EnSurv optimizes an energy score augmented with pooled Kaplan-Meier tail completion to handle right-censoring. We identify the pooled-completion target of the objective, establish consistency for its best approximation within the pre-ANM family, and give conditions and error bounds for its relation to true conditional graft survival. EnSurv also provides global explainability through summaries of fitted feature weights and incorporates domain constraints through established clinical directions using a soft gradient penalty. Extensive simulation experiments were conducted to evaluate the performance of the proposed method in comparison with existing methods. Application to the UNOS cohort demonstrates that EnSurv effectively models individual and region-level effects, consistently outperforming baselines across evaluation metrics encompassing discrimination, calibration, and accuracy.


### [480] [Latent Class Mixed Model Variational Autoencoders for Scalable Phenotyping of Health Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32391)
*Philomène Letzelter, Violaine Courrier, Sandrine Katsahian, Agathe Guilloux*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Clustering longitudinal health trajectories requires handling high-dimensional measurements, irregular visit times, and subject-level heterogeneity while retaining clinically interpretable structure. Existing methods address this only partially: latent-class mixed models yield an interpretable, covariate-driven partition but do not scale past a handful of markers, while deep time-series clustering scales at the cost of that structure and generally requires a common time grid. We introduce the Latent Class Mixed Model Variational Autoencoder (LCMM-VAE), a deep generative model whose latent prior is a mixture of class-specific linear mixed models, so that each subject's latent trajectory decomposes into its class mean trajectory and a subject-specific deviation. We derive a variational EM algorithm whose class responsibilities, random effects and mixed-model parameters are updated in closed form, and provide identifiability guarantees under stated conditions. Experiments on simulated and real longitudinal cohorts show that LCMM-VAE returns a partition, a reconstruction of incomplete trajectories and interpretable class-level dynamics at once, while remaining competitive with classical and deep baselines.


### [481] [PHM-ensemble: Scalable and Stable Probabilistic Multiresolution Clustering via Ensemble Density Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32945)
*Ali Turfah, Xiaoquan Wen*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Clustering is a foundational tool for exploratory data analysis across the sciences. We argue that clustering methods should be evaluated along four dimensions: fidelity to the data's underlying structure, interpretability of the recovered groups, stability under data perturbations and algorithmic randomness, and scalability to modern scientific datasets. Yet existing methods rarely satisfy all four criteria. To address this gap, we introduce PHM-ensemble, a general probabilistic framework for multiresolution clustering that estimates the underlying data density by pooling mixture models from an ensemble of fits and subsequently constructs a clustering hierarchy through hierarchical merging of the mixture components. On simulated datasets and the MNIST benchmark, PHM-ensemble matches or outperforms widely used clustering algorithms in agreement with ground truth, produces more stable results across runs, and reveals complex underlying structures. Applied to 1.82 million peripheral blood cells from the Human Immune Health Atlas, PHM-ensemble achieves higher agreement with expert-curated major cell-type annotations than the Leiden clustering used in the original study (adjusted Rand index 0.965 versus 0.789), while requiring only half the computing time. Beyond identifying major immune lineages, it reveals biologically coherent relationships among cell types and resolves finer structure within individual lineages, uncovering subtypes and cellular states consistent with known biology.


### [482] [Online V-Learning with Adaptive Longitudinal Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32947)
*Aniruddhan Ganesaraman, Michael R. Kosorok*

Main category: stat.ML

Abstract: Reinforcement learning methods for estimating optimal dynamic treatment regimes are increasingly applied in mobile health (mHealth), where treatment decisions are made continually. Luckett et al. (2020) proposed V-learning, which estimates an optimal policy from offline trajectories. In practice, mHealth policies are updated online. Batch updating, in which the policy is re-estimated after each block of new data, is a tractable middle ground between offline learning and updating after every observation. However, because each batch's treatment policy is estimated from data pooled across patients in earlier batches, the resulting data are adaptively collected and patient trajectories are dependent, a setting that offline theory does not address. We develop statistical theory for V-learning in this setting with varying, deterministic batch sizes. For each fixed batch, as the number of patients grows, we establish uniform consistency of the estimated value function, consistency of the estimated policy, a functional central limit theorem over the policy class, asymptotic normality of the estimated value of the learned policy, and a consistent variance estimator. To first order, the online procedure performs as well as an oracle that deploys the optimal policy after the first batch. With the number of patients fixed and the number of batches growing, the estimated value function and policy remain consistent under a persistent identifiability condition. In simulations, as the number of patients grows with the number of batches fixed, confidence intervals attain near-nominal coverage under adaptive assignment and the online procedure closely matches the oracle. With a small cohort followed over many batches, the learned policies improve steadily.


<div id='cs.DB'></div>

# cs.DB [[Back]](#toc)

### [483] [On the Creation of Object-Centric Event Logs from Patient Journeys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33074)
*Saimir Bala, Neven Borak, Maximilian König, Anjo Seidel, Zeinab Soleimani, Lorenzo Famiglini, Lev Petrov, Jonas Witt, Mathias Weske*

Main category: cs.DB

Abstract: Interactions between patients and health systems as well as conditions that individuals experience during a disease are represented in patient journeys. Therefore, patient journeys are important assets to investigate how care is being provided, and how health systems can be designed to better suit the needs of patients. While there is initial work on patient journeys from a process mining perspective with traditional case notions, object-centric process mining techniques have yet to be covered. This is unfortunate, since due to the rich structure of objects and their relationships, object-centric process mining approaches are particularly promising in this context. This paper addresses this gap by investigating object-centric event log (OCEL) generation from patient journeys. Based on a list of challenges and requirements, the paper presents an approach for the creation of OCELs from patient journey data. The approach is evaluated on a synthetic patient cohort provided by our industry partner.


<div id='eess.IV'></div>

# eess.IV [[Back]](#toc)

### [484] [Exact Volume-Preserving Diffeomorphic Shear Splitting: Continuous-Discrete Geometric Bounds, Reversible Memory Backpropagation, and Expressivity Frontiers in Volumetric Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31343)
*Alvaro Garcia-Casas*

Main category: eess.IV

Abstract: Incompressible diffeomorphic registration requires continuous spatial mappings that preserve local tissue volume (det(D\Phi) = 1) without topological folding (det(D\Phi) > 0). Standard velocity-field methods (SVF, LDDMM) approximate incompressibility via numerical ODE integration or soft penalties, exhibiting numerical volume drift (~10^-4 to 10^-1), iterative grid inversions, and linear backpropagation memory O(L * N_vox). We formulate an exact structure-preserving framework for 3D registration based on cascaded coordinate shears. By decomposing the divergence-free Lie algebra sdiff(\Omega) into six coordinate-shear generators whose convective non-linearities vanish identically, each elementary stage integrates analytically to a closed-form triangular shear with det(DS_k) = 1 and exact machine-precision inverse requiring zero iterations and zero spatial interpolations. Multi-stage compositions generate the connected volume-preserving group SDiff_0(\Omega). We establish: (i) an unconditional global diffeomorphism proof closing the Hadamard-Caccioppoli injectivity gap; (ii) a Dirichlet boundary confinement lemma; (iii) an anisotropic Riemannian similarity invariance; (iv) a Sobolev H^2 spectral budget preventing chaotic mixing; and (v) an exact reversible backpropagation engine eliminating O(L) trajectory storage (0.00 MB intermediate activations). We prove second-order O(h^2) sub-voxel convergence across 16^3 to 64^3 lattices and second-order trajectory accuracy for palindromic Strang splitting (468.1x error reduction vs. Lie-Trotter; drift |det(D\Phi)-1| <= 2.22e-16). Execution is 2.1x-4.0x faster than SVF with 78.4% GPU memory reduction. On cardiac cine-MRI (ACDC, N=100) and brain MRI (OASIS-1, N=414), our model achieves competitive Dice (0.884 and 0.841) while strictly maintaining 0.0000% folding and zero volume drift.


<div id='q-bio.BM'></div>

# q-bio.BM [[Back]](#toc)

### [485] [A Description-Derived Drug-Drug Interaction Benchmark Reveals Split-Dependent Molecular Representation Rankings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32371)
*Manel Gil-Sorribes, Isaac Filella-Merce, Alexis Molina*

Main category: q-bio.BM

Abstract: Drug-drug interaction (DDI) prediction is clinically important and poses a distinct challenge for molecular representation learning. Unlike single-molecule property prediction, DDI requires a predictor to compose two molecular representations into a relational outcome. DrugBank provides curator-authored DDI descriptions rather than ready-made categorical labels, so a categorical prediction target must first be derived from the text. We construct a dataset of DDI pairs by mapping processed descriptions to six text-derived categories and define a benchmark with four holdout regimes that restrict train-test chemical overlap from random pairs to scaffold-disjoint evaluation. We then use this benchmark to compare molecular fingerprints, pretrained sequence representations, and graph-derived features across five seeds and multiple classifier heads. Molecular fingerprints lead the high- overlap random split, whereas graph-derived representations lead the drug- and scaffold-holdout regimes, with the gap increasing as molecular overlap is restricted. Changing the classifier head affects absolute performance but not the relative representation ranking. These results show that representation rankings change with test-set novelty under a fixed description-derived label space.


<div id='physics.chem-ph'></div>

# physics.chem-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [486] [Path-probability gradients for optimizing force fields against dynamical observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32851)
*Zhongyao Wang, Lun Song, Yakun Zeng, Xingao Gong, Rui Shi, Mao Su*

Main category: physics.chem-ph

Abstract: Machine learning force fields inherit the errors of the electronic-structure methods used to generate their training data, and refining them against experimental measurements provides a direct route beyond this limit. However, such refinement remains difficult for dynamical observables because differentiating chaotic molecular trajectories yields unstable gradients. Here, we introduce PathGrad, which differentiates Langevin path probabilities instead of the trajectories themselves. The resulting gradient estimator is accumulated on the fly from thermal noise and force derivatives, and its variance remains stable as the trajectory length increases. We validated the gradient estimator against finite-difference calculations and used it to fine-tune molten-copper and water potentials against experimental diffusion coefficients together with structural constraints. For water, jointly optimizing diffusion and the oxygen-oxygen radial distribution function also improves the predictions of shear viscosity and rotational relaxation, neither of which is included in the optimization objective. Alignment at a single temperature also improves the apparent activation energies of multiple dynamical observables, bringing their temperature dependences closer to experiments. Moreover, the optimized potential exhibits smaller force errors relative to an independent, higher-level quantum-chemical reference, indicating that alignment with experiments improves the fidelity of the potential energy surface beyond the fitted targets. PathGrad thus establishes a general framework for incorporating experimental observables directly into force field optimizations.
